# Intake ledger: every return, whether it has been taken in, by whom, and what is waiting

Built 26 Nov 2026 13:15 UTC by `field_experts/build_intake_ledger.py` (a script, no model; rebuilt by `update_registry.py`). Read `INTAKE_LEDGER_README.md` for the rule: **before launching any intake or expert read, rebuild this ledger and read the run's row.** 'Read up to' comes from the intake documents (the checkpoints they name, the return folders they cite) and from the authoring agents' own transcripts (the return folders their tool calls touched); '(by date)' marks a document that names no checkpoint, credited with the newest return older than itself, which does not count as a read. Pro = GPT-6 Astra (ChatGPT Pro mode).

**Research runs: 215**: taken in up to the latest 85, partial 1, not taken in 129.  **Transcription units: 252** (second pass not prepared 143, second pass not started 55, second pass partial 29, not needed: transcription with second pass 21, running or stalled 2, not needed: control run 2).  Reader editions: 8.

## 0. What has been taken in, statement by statement (the record; the document-based status of sections 1-2 is an indication only)

A statement of a run's newest register counts as taken in when `results_register/CHECK_REGISTER.tsv` holds a check event on it by an intake or expert (a Claude or Fable checker), not the run's own in-run events. A document that names a run or a checkpoint is not a check. Rebuild order: `results_register/build_check_register.py`, then this script. Known limit: an intake that covers two runs in one file (SA22 with SA22_run2) files its events under the first run's name.

**Statements in the newest registers: 11137. Checked by an intake: 6853. Not yet checked: 4284.** The unchecked ids, run by run, are in section 9.

### 1b. Taken in up to the newest, but thinly: the readers name under a quarter of the newest register

## 2. Pro intake-prompt candidates, in order

An intake can be a Pro prompt: the returns as one package, "read all of it, sort it, find the mathematics". These are the runs not taken in up to the latest whose unread part is large (40 or more register rows), so the job is mostly reading and sorting. Order: most unread rows first. After each Pro intake the field's Fable expert weighs in for the programme (step 3 of the field-expert workflow). The other runs in section 1 are small or have no register (papers): they go to a Fable intake or straight to the field expert, because the work is judgement on what matters.

| # | Run | Programme | Unread | Newest | Expert to weigh in after |
|---:|---|---|---:|---|---|
| 1 | FINMON_CP2 | Archive research (sweep 3) | ~286 rows | cp12 (25 Nov 13:45 UTC) | none |

### 2b. Pro candidates for the rest of the thinly read runs (section 1b), in order

The rows no intake names are not proved unread: an intake may have read a row without naming its id. So each such prompt is briefed with the earlier intakes (as background, named in the prompt) and asked to sort the whole register, marking the rows the earlier intakes did not treat.

## 4. Transcription runs

Pro packages: the second pass (Claude, text against the scans, blocks in `second_pass/prompts` vs `second_pass/out`) and whether the package's research notes were read by the notes readers (FOLLOWUPS/CLAIMS/GAP_FROM_NOTES_20261117). Codex web (GPT-6 Astra at reasoning effort max) by cote: its Pro second pass (SP prompts), which adds the commentary.

Done so far: notes passes cover 50 packages (follow-ups), 50 (claims), 62 (gap); Claude second pass output for 18 packages; control verdicts on 4 units. The method audits are `grothendieck_archive_transcription_20261110/second_pass/METHOD_AUDIT_20261117.md` (the Claude second pass) and `codex_web_20261113/METHOD_AUDIT_AND_PLAN_20261117.md` (the Codex-web route).

## 6. Our own notes: checked by a session other than their author?

Every intake, expert, consult, referee, debate and findings note of ours, and the main session's notes (claude_math_notes). Pro runs read many of them as background to set their directions, but reading is not checking. 'Checked' = a check event in results_register/CHECK_REGISTER.tsv by a session other than the note's author names it, or a Pro prompt names it in a paragraph that asks for checking, or the prompt is a check prompt, or CROSS_MODEL_CATCHES.md names it. Which model ran a check does not matter; how it was done is in its record. 'Sent as input' = the note was in a prompt's zip or text without a check request. Notes with no check on record are candidates for one Pro check prompt per field: "check every mathematical claim in these notes".
